Coupled-market model: notes, 2026-11-16 (second set; follows coupled_market_notes.txt)
====================================================================================

Question worked on: the February 2026 fit applied unchanged to March-September drifts to +6 ct by August (version 10).
Is the supply side stable once decomposed, and if not, which part moves?

1. Plants taken one at a time, no market model (exp/drift_look*.py, out/drift_look*.png)
   - Gas: utilisation against (price - 1.85 gas - 0.37 carbon) is one curve for all six periods mid-2024 .. Nov 2026.
   - Lignite, hard coal: five periods coincide after subtracting carbon; Aug-Nov 2026 departs in the upper part
     (half-utilisation margin moves from about 0 to about +30 EUR/MWh; 85% is reached only near +100).
   - Price-free view (drift_look4.png): at a given thermal output above must-run, the mix of lignite / hard coal / gas
     is the same in Aug-Nov 2024, 2025, 2026. The price attached differs: 85-88 EUR/MWh at 9 GW in 2024/25, 136 in 2026.
   - Not outages (ENTSO-E unavailability, out/de_outage_hourly.parquet: lignite out 0.8-2.0 GW in those months, as before).
   - Not ramping (same on flat price stretches), not midday/solar (night, shoulder, midday all show it).
   - Unit level (ENTSO-E generation per unit, exp/unit_look.py): the same units (Lippendorf R/S, Schwarze Pumpe A/B,
     Boxberg, Neurath F/G, Weisweiler ...) ran at ~0.9 load at prices 20-60 above carbon in Oct 2025 and at 0.35-0.85
     (fleet 0.65-0.69) in Oct 2026; units stay on and throttle. All owners.
   - 2021-2023 (exp/hist_build.py, hist_look.py, out/hist2123.parquet): Jul-Dec 2021 had the same gap between gas-fired
     and lignite cost as late 2026 (about 80 EUR/MWh) and lignite ran at 0.83-0.89 at margins 10-120. 2022 shows a milder
     stretch (0.67-0.77 at margins 10-60). So expensive gas alone does not produce the 2026 pattern.
   - Cause not identified. Hard coal fuel price is a candidate for hard coal only (World Bank monthly: coal went from
     10 to 40 EUR/MWh thermal in 2022, i.e. +70 EUR/MWh electric at 40% efficiency; the file ends Dec 2024, no 2026 value).
   - Why the forward check fails: until Feb 2026 gas, hard coal and lignite all cost 85-95 EUR/MWh electric, so nothing
     in that window shows how solid-fuel plants offer once the costs separate; the prior was a pure merit order.

2. Heat-led plants (user: "coal often means Heizkraftwerk"), exp/drift_look5.py, out/drift_look5.png
   - Output in hours when running clearly does not pay: gas 1.4 GW on mild days, 7.1 GW below 3 C; hard coal 0.8 / 2.9.
   - Output in hours when it clearly pays: hard coal 3.3 GW (13-18 C) to 6.2 GW (< 3 C); the same line in every period.

3. Borders (exp/border_look.py)
   - German wind explains 9-36% of the scheduled exchange with AT, CZ, PL, CH at a given price difference; sun and wind
     30-37% with DK; under 7% with NL, FR, BE.
   - NL: the 10-90% band of the exchange is 4 GW wide at any price difference, and exports occur against the price
     difference: under flow-based coupling the bilateral exchange is not a function of the bilateral price difference.

4. Version 11 (exp/star.py; version 10 kept as star_v10.py): must-run of German lignite / hard coal / gas = f0 + f1 * heating
   degrees; each zone's demand for its price-driven stack falls by hz * heating degrees; export and import capacity per
   border times exp(a_wind * wind + a_sun * sun).
   fit date        in-sample MAE   latent cost level   v10 for comparison
   2026-09-18      1.27 ct         -18..+17            1.31 ct, -34..+22
   2026-02-02      1.22 ct          -6..+16            1.20 ct,   0..+28
   must-run: gas 0.8 GW + 0.26-0.34 per degree, hard coal 0.5 + 0.09, lignite 2.8-3.1 + 0.01-0.04.
   neighbours' heat-led output per degree: FR 0.8-1.1 (far above its prior of 0.1: absorbing something seasonal in the
   French stack, not CHP), NL 0.2, PL 0.24, CZ 0.09, DK 0.05, AT/CH 0.
   forward, cost level frozen: Feb -> Mar..Sep 2026 2.56 ct (v10 3.24), month errors +0.3 +1.1 +0.5 +2.2 +2.8 +2.7 +4.2 +4.8;
   Sep -> 6 Nov 2.26 ct (v10 2.08), gas 1.5 GW under.

5. Simulated-data check, generative order (exp/star_sim.py v11 autumn 2026-09-18): shocks -> clearing -> prices and quantities -> refit.
   Recovered: German thermal blocks (capacity, cost, width, heat rates), inertia, must-run and heat coefficients, storage,
   all exchange parameters incl. weather, latent cost path (correlation 0.99). Recovered model against the simulated price: 0.17 ct;
   the model's own shocks move the price by 1.20 ct, the size of the in-sample error on real data.
   Not recovered separately: German low-cost block width and the elastic slope (they trade off); neighbours' individual
   gas capacities. Lignite's gas link came back as 0.42 for a true 0.56 (pulled toward its prior of 0).
   Model world against data: hours within 0.5 EUR/MWh of a neighbour's price 2-8% simulated, 14-54% observed. The smooth
   exchange function does not generate price equality.

6. Per-zone neighbour forecasts (Timestral, zone by zone with own weather), autumn 2026, 66 forecasts:
   neighbours' residual load error first 14 h 4.07 -> 3.85 GW (-6%); France 2.31 and Poland 2.07 GW dominate.
   structural model + online correction, night / first 14 h: 1.53 / 1.78 (per-zone) vs 1.58 / 1.82 (one sum) vs 1.59 / 1.85
   (no neighbours); ceiling with published neighbours 1.45 / 1.72. Averaged with Timestral price: 1.30 / 1.62 (was 1.31 / 1.63).
   Summer and winter still running (queue restarted after a worker restart).

7. Versions 12-16 (same day, later)
   v12  German lignite / hard coal offer premium as their own weekly states (not loading on gas); heating threshold estimated;
        market-expected residual load = published + theta * (realised - published), theta 0.58-0.62; additive wind / sun shift of exchanges.
        In sample 1.25 / 1.19 ct. Forward: Feb -> Mar..Sep 2.52, Sep -> 6 Nov 2.20.
   v13  exchange curve of each border learned from the scheduled exchange alone (no gradient from balances or Jacobian); heating threshold per zone.
        Dutch bounds +1.8 / -3.5 GW (joint fit had +4.3 / -6.5; exchange data alone +1.9 / -3.1), CZ +0.8 / -0.6, AT +2.1 / -0.6;
        weather shifts AT +0.45, CZ +0.49, PL +0.23 GW per sd wind, DK +0.59 per sd sun. German must-run threshold 12-14 C.
        In sample 1.33 / 1.22 ct (slightly worse: the inflated bounds had been compensating for the level problem).
   Level test (exp/passthru.py): Aug-Nov 2025 -> Aug-Nov 2026 the price rose 72 EUR/MWh (1.67 per EUR of gas); block models 1.1-1.2.
        In sample the two-month block errors run -0.3..-0.7 ct in 2025 and +0.9..+1.5 over the last six months: the level does not flow
        through the functions. (User: "price level should flow through functions of params not through params directly".)
   Ratio law (exp/ratio_law.py, ratio_law2.py; out/ratio_law*.png): price / (1.85 gas + 0.37 carbon) by residual-load bin, twelve half-years
        2021-2026 (gas 22..158): spread 8% above 35 GW (price itself 44-51%); against residual load minus available nuclear + lignite +
        hard coal: 5-7% for x >= 10 GW, 11% for 0-10 GW. The cheap fleet available fell from 31 GW (2021) to 17 GW.
   v14  costs without free levels (heat rate * gas + EF * carbon + small term; coal price; water values as a share of gas-fired cost),
        widths proportional to cost. No improvement on its own (response per EUR of gas 0.98).
   v15  solid-fuel offers = own cost + share * gap to gas-fired cost (share a weekly state, prior 0.15). Shares came out 0.4-0.6; response 1.08.
   v16  two layers (user: "latent layer that captures merit order price mechanism"): the offer stack sets the price and is identified from
        the balances at observed prices and the Jacobian only; a separate dispatch layer explains measured plant output in relative price
        units. No latent level state at all.
        Dispatch layer: half loading at price / gas-fired cost 0.72 lignite, 0.68 hard coal, 1.04 gas; one curve over both price levels.
        Stack (Sep fit): 5.7 GW solid fuel at own cost (narrow), 11 GW at gas-fired cost, 6.5 GW at 1.4x; a very wide catch-all block.
        Level change 2025 -> 2026 from costs alone, by load bin (>45, 35-45, 25-35, 15-25, 5-15 GW): data +122 +103 +81 +70 +83;
        v13 +109 +81 +61 +50 +33; v16 +125 +95 +70 +49 +23. In sample 1.32 / 1.24 ct. Forward Sep -> 6 Nov 2.07, Feb -> Sep 2.42.
        Still wrong below 25 GW (Aug-Nov 2026 medians: 5-15 GW model 72 vs 102; 15-25 GW 128 vs 146), and the Feb fit cannot tell how much
        solid fuel is offered at own cost (10.3 GW vs 5.7) because all three fuels cost the same in its window.
   Low load (exp/lowload_look.py): at German residual load 5-15 GW the price / gas-fired cost rose 0.07 -> 0.60 (2024H1 -> 2026H2); the
        neighbours' prices rose alike (FR 0.03 -> 0.59, NL 0.07 -> 0.63), German net exports +3.6 -> +5.8 GW, thermal output +3.4 GW.
8. Forecasts from the coupled-market model, autumn 2026, 66 origins, weekly refits (exp/star_fc.py, star_fc2.py), night / first 14 h MAE ct:
        v12 blocks: published RL 1.72 / 2.03 (bias +1.05), Timestral RL 1.93 / 2.36; with an online level correction 1.48 / 1.71, 1.73 / 2.12
        v16 two layers: published RL 1.77 / 2.06 (bias +1.13), Timestral RL 2.01 / 2.46; with online correction 1.58 / 1.80, 1.89 / 2.30
        for reference: earlier structural curve alone 1.69 / 1.98; with online correction and per-zone neighbours 1.53 / 1.78;
        Timestral price 1.41 / 1.84; trees 1.39 / 1.65; candidate average 1.31 / 1.63.
        The night bias is the level deficit below 35 GW, where most night hours of that season sit.
9. Per-zone neighbour forecasts: summer finished (50 origins); winter running.
Open: upper solid-fuel segment positioned in units of gas-fired cost (max(own cost, a * L)); the surplus-to-solid-fuel transition at low
load; prior for the solid-fuel capacity offered at own cost (unit data: minimum-load share); storage and exchange scales against
relative price (not yet drawn); neighbours' stacks in the same two-layer form; simulated-data check for the two-layer model (star_sim.py
still reads parameters of version 11).

10. After review (same day): corrections and the married model
   Corrections to section 7: v16 did not pass the named tests (forecast bias +1.13 ct, in-sample late blocks +1.1/+1.5/+1.0, Feb forward +4.6 ct in
   the last month; Sep forward 2.07 = v10's 2.08). Its own 2025->2026 change by load bin is +111 +92 +71 +50 +25 (the +125.. row was costs alone),
   and below 25 GW part of the data's change is quantity (exports +2.3 GW, thermal +3.4 GW at the same load). The reading of the v16 stack as plant
   behaviour is withdrawn: a second seed gives the same prices (0.12 ct apart) and dispatch layer but different blocks (capacities 5.7/5.7/11.0/6.5
   vs 5.2/3.8/12.4/16.0 GW). Only the aggregate curve is identified.
   Stack target, model-free (exp/stack_target.py): price against measured thermal output (lignite + hard coal + gas) by period. At 9 GW the price is
   0.78-0.93 of lignite's cost in every period outside winter; from 13 GW up it is 0.9-1.2 of the gas-fired cost (absolute: 79 -> 136 EUR/MWh at 13 GW).
   v17 (stack tied to summed thermal output at observed price): no improvement (in sample 1.37 / 1.22, late blocks +1.2..+1.9, Sep forward 2.53).
   v18 (second solid segment at max(own cost, aB * gas-fired cost)): see q854.log.
   Married model (user: "learn the shape of the order curve and a demand proxy and then marry the two"), exp/oc.py, 18 s per fit:
     curve:  x = thermal - mustrun(T); w = sigmoid((x - cw * solid availability - qw) / ww); p = (1 - w) * a(x) * lignite cost + w * b(x) * gas-fired cost
     proxy:  thermal = mustrun + softplus(k0 + k1 RL + k2 (RL - day mean) + neighbours' RL + calendar + wind/sun [+ k4 log(gas-fired / lignite cost) + k5 FR nuclear])
     Separate fits: the curve given measured thermal output has no drift over the window (errors by ninth scattered around zero); the price from
     the drivers drifts +1.1 / +2.3 ct in the last two ninths. So the level problem is in the demand proxy (Germany runs more thermal at the same
     residual load in 2026, i.e. exports), not in the price mechanism. With the cost ratio in the proxy (+6..+9 GW per unit log ratio) the in-sample
     drift disappears, but the term is not identified in the February window (+1.0) and overshoots out of sample.
     Forward: Feb -> Sep 2.18 ct (bias +1.40) with cost ratio, 2.28 without; Sep -> 6 Nov 2.54 (bias -1.31) with, 1.91 (+0.65) without.
     Forecasts, autumn 66 origins, weekly refits, no state, no online layer, night / first 14 h:
        joint fit without cost ratio: Timestral RL 1.74 / 2.02 (bias +0.26), published RL 1.68 / 1.86 (bias +0.36)
        joint fit with cost ratio:    2.44 / 2.56 (bias -1.56)  -> rejected
     In the joint fit the proxy is no longer thermal output (measured thermal is 2-4 GW above it in Sep-Nov; the curve at measured thermal
     overpredicts by 4.5 ct), so it is a price index anchored loosely to a quantity.
   Hourly models scored on quarter-hours lose 0.49 ct at night / 0.72 ct over the first 14 h in this period by construction.
   Per-zone neighbour forecasts, summer (50 origins): neighbours' residual-load error -15%; structural 0.87 / 1.28 vs 0.89 / 1.30 (one sum); the
   averaged candidate unchanged within noise (0.81 / 1.32 vs 0.79 / 1.28).

11. Final state of the day (after a second review)
   v18 (second solid segment at max(own cost, aB * gas-fired cost)): in sample 1.37 / 1.22 ct, late blocks +1.2 / +1.7 / +1.1, response to gas 1.11
   per EUR (data 1.67), Sep forward 2.38, Feb forward 2.45. No block version (v10-v18) passed the level test.
   Married model, further checks:
     - separate fits, no cost ratio, autumn forecasts: 2.52 / 2.66 (bias +1.34); the curve at MEASURED thermal output is 3.3 ct too high out of
       sample while the proxy is 4.3 GW below measured thermal. Both halves are off, in opposite directions. The earlier statement that the level
       problem is "not in the price mechanism" rested on in-sample errors and is withdrawn.
     - the cost-ratio term is withdrawn as an explanation: coefficient +6.0 / +8.9 GW (Sep fits) vs +1.0 / +3.7 (Feb fits); gas rose almost
       monthly from June 2026, so it cannot be told from a trend; in forecast mode it overshoots (2.44 / 2.56, bias -1.56). The French nuclear
       input used availability at the forecast hour (not known at the origin) and had the wrong sign. The fact remains: in 2026 Germany runs
       3-6 GW more thermal at the same residual load (net exports).
     - joint fit without cost ratio: autumn 1.74 / 2.02 (Timestral RL), summer 1.28 / 1.81; the earlier free-form curve alone is 1.69 / 1.98 and
       0.98 / 1.55. Chosen from two variants on the same 66 origins. It is a latent index in new units, not two separately checkable halves.
     - on hourly means, autumn: married 1.62 / 1.81; structural + online 1.47 / 1.72; Timestral 1.36 / 1.79; trees 1.33 / 1.57; candidate 1.27 / 1.58.
   Bottom line: the working forecaster is unchanged (candidate average: autumn 1.31 / 1.63, summer 0.79 / 1.28 ct). Nothing built today beats it.
   What held up as description of the market: heat-led must-run as a function of temperature; exchange bounds and weather shifts from the scheduled
   exchanges; market-expected residual load 60% of the way from published to realised; price / gas-fired cost stable to 5-11% once the available
   cheap capacity is netted out (2021-2026); unit-level throttling of German lignite and hard coal in 2026 (cause unknown); the regional quantity
   shift of 2026 (more German thermal at the same residual load), which no demand side built so far captures.

12. SMARD and the axis tests (user pointed to www.smard.de and api.energy-charts.info)
   SMARD catalogue (ext/smard/catalog.json; exp/smard_catalog.py, smard_fetch.py): realised generation by source; installed capacity; day-ahead and
   intraday forecasts (wind, PV, total, "other"); forecast and realised load and residual load; prices for 17 zones incl. IT North, NO2, SE4, SI, HU;
   scheduled commercial exchange and physical flow per border incl. Norway, Sweden, Luxembourg; balancing volumes and prices; redispatch and
   countertrading costs; and a plant list of 596 plants / 1956 blocks with status, CHP flag, capacity, commissioning year and per-block production
   and outage series.  In market operation: hard coal 8.6 GW (7.9 GW flagged CHP), gas 25.3 GW (19.6 CHP), lignite 14.8 GW; in grid / KVBG reserve
   and so outside the market: hard coal 6.6 GW, gas 2.5 GW plus 1.4 GW capacity reserve; batteries 1.5 GW listed.
   Axis test, model-free (exp/axis_test.py, axis_test2.py): mean absolute deviation of the German price from its median within 2 GW bins, per quarter:
     published residual load 1.56 ct; realised residual load 1.53; realised thermal output 2.02; thermal + other + pumped generation 1.67;
     SMARD day-ahead scheduled generation other than wind and PV 1.87; residual load + 0.3 x neighbours' residual loads 1.37.
     Adding measured net exports or pumped storage to the axis makes it worse (outcomes, not drivers).
   One curve for Jul 2024 - Nov 2026: raw price on German residual load 2.60 ct; price / gas-fired cost on the regional index shifted by solid-fuel
   availability, heating degrees and French nuclear availability 1.61 ct.
   Index model (exp/ocx.py; specification fixed before scoring; 15 s per fit; no state): index = German residual load + weights on eight neighbours
   - solid availability - heat - French nuclear + calendar; price = blend of lignite cost and gas-fired cost with learned curve shapes.
     in sample 1.26 / 1.33 ct (Sep / Feb fit); forward Sep -> 6 Nov 2.03 ct with bias +0.14 and no drift by month (+0.25, -0.01);
     forward Feb -> Sep 1.96 ct, bias +1.42, still rising to +2.8 by the last month.
     forecasts, weekly refits: autumn 1.68 / 1.95 (Timestral RL), 1.58 / 1.81 (published RL); summer 1.18 / 1.68, 1.02 / 1.53.
     Earlier free-form curve alone: autumn 1.69 / 1.98, summer 0.98 / 1.55. Candidate average unchanged and ahead: 1.31 / 1.63, 0.79 / 1.28.

13. The three follow-ups on the index model (user: "ok go for it")
   Screening on data before 2026-03-31 only, two-half stability test (exp/screen.py; target price / gas-fired cost; out-of-half MAE change in ct):
     pass: neighbours' nuclear availability pooled FR+BE+CH+CZ+NL, 14-day max (-0.12 / -0.26); German lignite + hard coal 14-day max (-0.15 / -0.24);
           German gas, last known day's max (-0.03 / -0.06); Germany's own D+1 residual (-0.34 / -0.52).
     fail: French nuclear alone; the other neighbours' nuclear alone; run-of-river AT+CH+FR+DE; every D+1 spread to a ring zone (FR NL BE AT PL CZ CH DK,
           northern Italy, southern Norway) and the pooled south / west / north / east spreads.
     ceiling: German outages by fuel with hindsight -0.08 / -0.08 ct. Publication times are not recoverable (ENTSO-E records hold only the latest
           revision: 47 of 14,928 outages have more than one; SMARD per-block series are plain value series), so "availability known in advance" is closed.
   Index model v2 (exp/ocx2.py): pooled nuclear + gas availability added, French-only term dropped, all availabilities built from days <= d-3.
     in sample 1.27 ct; forward Feb -> Sep 1.91 (bias +1.33), Sep -> 6 Nov 2.07 (bias +0.42).
     forecasts (Timestral RL), night / first 14 h:        summer        autumn        winter (inside the screening window)
       v1                                                 1.18 / 1.68   1.68 / 1.95   1.47 / 1.27
       v2                                                 1.20 / 1.72   1.68 / 1.99   1.44 / 1.25
       v2 + persistence of the D+1 relative error (0.5)   1.09 / 1.59   1.72 / 2.02   1.30 / 1.13
       v2 + hour-specific correction in price             1.05 / 1.73   1.83 / 2.10   1.49 / 1.28
       v2 + hour-specific correction in index space       1.00 / 1.61   1.84 / 2.10   1.21 / 1.10
       earlier structural curve alone                     0.98 / 1.55   1.69 / 1.98   1.41 / 1.22
       earlier structural + its online correction         0.87 / 1.28   1.53 / 1.78   0.87 / 0.91
       trees                                              0.89 / 1.31   1.39 / 1.65   0.72 / 0.82
       candidate average                                  0.79 / 1.28   1.31 / 1.63   0.67 / 0.88
     The summer bias of the index model (+0.6..+1.2 ct from May to August, at 15-45 GW and all night hours) is not moved by the new terms.
     Three online layers were tried on the same origins; none is robust across periods (all hurt in autumn).
   Hourly against quarter-hourly scoring is not the explanation: on hourly means the index model alone is 1.09 / 1.50 (summer), 1.54 / 1.72 (autumn),
   1.39 / 1.20 (winter) against 0.94 / 1.49, 1.63 / 1.92, 1.37 / 1.18 for the earlier curve alone.
   Per-zone neighbour forecasts, winter: neighbours' residual-load error -12%, price forecast not improved (0.87 / 0.91 vs 0.82 / 0.86 one sum).
   Correction to the table above: the "earlier structural curve alone" row is the run without neighbours. Like for like (earlier curve with per-zone
   neighbours, no online layer) it is 0.93 / 1.49 (summer), 1.63 / 1.90 (autumn), 1.19 / 1.10 (winter), so the index model is behind in all three
   periods on quarter-hours (on hourly means: 0.89 / 1.44, 1.57 / 1.84, 1.15 / 1.06 against 1.11 / 1.54, 1.54 / 1.77, 1.36 / 1.17). The earlier model's
   online layer gains 6% / 6% / 27% at night like for like; the index-space port gains 17% in summer, 16% in winter and loses 10% in autumn.
   Version 2 of the index model is a bundle (pooled nuclear, gas availability, French term dropped, lag change); the bundle changed nothing.
   Index weights are not stable between fits (Swiss 1.46 vs 0.44, Czech 0.40 vs 0.94, nuclear 0.41 vs 0.18; heat threshold 19-20 C against a prior
   of 13 +- 2.5) and should not be read as a description of the region. The ring screen covered northern Italy and southern Norway only.
   Bias by month of origin, first 14 h, summer period (Apr May Jun Jul Aug): earlier curve with neighbours -0.50 +0.74 +0.73 +0.54 +1.19;
   index model -0.70 +0.92 +0.89 +0.81 +1.21; earlier with its online layer -0.78 +0.05 +0.16 -0.03 +0.32. Both curves miss the same level effect
   from May 2026; the earlier model's drift state does not carry it, its online layer does.

14. Per-block data (SMARD) and the owner question (user: market power factor or CO2? "we don't have to care about the interpretation to recognize that a
    per owner structure / term is relevant")
   Data: production per block, hourly, Jul 2024 - 11 Nov 2026 for 57 lignite / hard coal blocks >= 100 MW and 17 gas blocks >= 300 MW (ext/smard/blocks;
   exp/smard_blocks_fetch*.py). Published five days late. Blocks sum to 7.8 of 7.9 GW of lignite output and 2.5 of 3.2 GW of hard coal.
   In market operation: lignite LEAG 6.0, RWE 5.7, EnBW 1.8, Saale 0.9 GW; hard coal EnBW 2.4 GW and seven other owners 4.5 GW.
   Output at the same margin over fuel and carbon cost, Aug - 8 Nov, 2025 -> 2026 (exp/owner_look.py; out/owner_fig.png), margin 30-60 EUR/MWh:
     RWE lignite 4.3 -> 3.4 GW (share on 0.80 -> 0.72, loading of on-blocks 0.95 -> 0.84; unchanged above 120); EnBW lignite 1.3 -> 0.9; Saale 0.5 -> 0.4;
     LEAG lignite 3.4 -> 3.5 (more blocks on, each loaded less); EnBW hard coal 1.2 -> 1.1; other hard coal 1.9 -> 2.1; RWE gas (Emsland) 0.8 -> 0.5;
     other large gas blocks 3.0 -> 3.1.   Apr - Jul: RWE lignite 4.0 -> 3.2, LEAG 3.2 -> 3.3; RWE gas 0.8 -> 0.1; other gas 2.2 -> 3.0.
     Reported unavailability (ENTSO-E): RWE lignite Apr-Jul 0.72 -> 1.37 GW (planned), Aug-Nov 1.07 -> 1.06; LEAG 0.2 -> 0.2.
     So the earlier "all owners hold back" (one October fortnight) and "hard coal holds back" (fleet utilisation of a trailing maximum) are withdrawn.
     Relative to their own 14-day maximum both owner classes deliver less in autumn 2026 (held back +0.7..+1.3 GW each); in absolute output at the same
     margin the large portfolios (RWE, EnBW) are down about 1 GW and the others flat to up.
   Not CO2: Czech lignite and Polish hard coal (same carbon price) show no shift; German gas is on one curve; the block shifts do not order by age.
   Market power: the owner pattern is what unilateral withholding would look like; owner-specific constraints cannot be excluded from dispatch data.
   Tests of states built from this (index model, weekly refits, Timestral RL; night / first 14 h):          summer        autumn        winter
     v2 (fleet solid-fuel 14-day max)                                                                      1.20 / 1.72   1.68 / 1.99   1.44 / 1.25
     lignite entered as delivered when in the money (fleet, lag 3)                                         1.22 / 1.74   1.72 / 1.98   1.46 / 1.27
     solid fuel by owner class, delivered when in the money (lag 7)                                        1.25 / 1.78   1.85 / 2.10   1.48 / 1.28
     In the owner-class model the weight on the large portfolios' supply is 0.22 fitted to March 2026 and 1.49 fitted to September 2026.
     Pre-summer screening: fleet "held back" fails; owner-class outputs add nothing stable once heat and fleet availability are in the baseline.
     Fleet-level held back by month (GW), 2025 -> 2026: May 2.0 -> 1.9, Jun 2.8 -> 2.0, Jul 1.7 -> 2.2, Aug 1.5 -> 2.6, Sep 1.9 -> 2.9, Oct 1.4 -> 2.6.
   Oracle on the switch point between solid-fuel and gas pricing (fitted at 23-25 GW before 2026, 2-8 GW with 2026 in the window): fixing the curve shape
     at the September values and refitting the rest on the February window lowers the forward bias Mar-Sep from +1.33 to +0.96 ct. It is a minor part.
   Corrections after review (section 14):
   - The owner contrast is thinner than the figure suggests. At a margin of 30-60 the loading of ONLINE blocks fell for every lignite owner (RWE 0.95 -> 0.84,
     EnBW 0.95 -> 0.86, LEAG 0.83 -> 0.77, Saale 0.84 -> 0.66). LEAG's flat output comes from more blocks on (0.68 -> 0.76; two of its blocks ran only 26-27% of
     the 2025 season); about half of RWE's drop is fewer blocks on (0.80 -> 0.72). Lignite has no independent owner, so owner and fuel are confounded; the only
     within-fuel comparison is hard coal (EnBW's blocks 1.2 -> 1.1 GW against the other owners' 1.9 -> 2.1; in Apr-Jul the others are down too, 1.9 -> 1.7).
     Market power is not excluded and not shown.
   - The size estimate ("1.3-1.6 GW less lignite is the same order as the missing 0.5-1.2 ct") and "most of the miss is on the quantity side" are withdrawn:
     in May and June both curves run 0.7-0.9 ct low while solid fuel delivered when in the money is unchanged (large portfolios 5.4 -> 5.0 and 4.1 -> 4.1 GW,
     others 5.1 -> 5.2 and 4.5 -> 4.7). The switch-point test rules out curve shape for about 0.4 ct and does not locate the rest.
   - Lignite effect at its properly measured size (blocks, fixed block maxima, Aug - 8 Nov): fleet loading 0.66 -> 0.60 at margin 30-60, 0.69 -> 0.68 at 60-120,
     0.72 -> 0.83 above 120. The earlier "+30 EUR/MWh shift" used utilisation of a trailing maximum.
   - Cross-country with a fixed denominator (mean output / 99.5th percentile, by margin band 0-30, 30-60, 60-120, above 120; 2025 -> 2026):
       German lignite 0.62 0.70 0.74 0.77 -> 0.50 0.58 0.68 0.84;  Polish lignite 0.56 0.67 0.73 0.76 -> 0.50 0.63 0.69 0.77;
       Czech lignite 0.53 0.60 0.63 0.70 -> 0.55 0.61 0.67 0.80;   German hard coal 0.36 0.46 0.54 0.59 -> 0.31 0.44 0.50 0.56;
       Polish hard coal 0.47 0.55 0.60 0.65 -> 0.49 0.54 0.59 0.68.
     German lignite 12 points lower up to a margin of 60 and 7 higher above 120; Polish lignite 4-6 lower; Czech lignite and Polish hard coal unchanged.
   - Control for the owner-class model: the same blocks, lag and statistic as ONE term gives 1.27 / 1.80 (summer), 1.81 / 2.05 (autumn), 1.48 / 1.29 (winter);
     split by owner class 1.25 / 1.78, 1.85 / 2.10, 1.48 / 1.28. The split itself changes nothing measurable (-2%, +2%, 0%); the loss against v2 comes from the
     older and noisier state (lag 7, in-the-money mean). The large-portfolio weight moving from 0.22 to 1.49 between fits is instability, not evidence.


15. LEVEL AS A LATENT STATE WITH MISSING DATA (state-space layer for the index model)
=====================================================================================
Idea (user): use the data where they exist and impute where they do not. At a forecast origin the level error of the curve is observed, noisily, for
every day up to D+1 (prices known), and missing for D+2. Earlier online layers used fixed memories and no notion of how noisy a day's evidence is.

15.1 Specification, written down BEFORE any forecast was scored (2026-11-16)
  Evidence   per hour with a known price: the shift of the load index [GW] at which the fitted curve gives that price (bisection, +-15 GW; a hit at the
             bound, or a missing input, makes the hour missing). Per day: the median over its hours, at least 12 valid hours, else the day is missing.
             Loads as published, no realised-load term (what exists at an origin), unless the size check says the realised-load term matters.
  State      z_d = m_d + u_d + noise;  m_d = m_{d-1} + xi (slow level, random walk);  u_d = phi * u_{d-1} + eta (fast part, reverts to zero).
             The data before each forecast period decide the four variances / persistence (maximum likelihood with weak, untruncated priors), including
             whether there is a slow level at all (var(xi) -> 0) or only a part that reverts to zero.
  Learning   out of sample, the way the layer is used: weekly fits of the index model on a fixed calendar; for each fit the evidence over the 90 days
             before the fit date (warm-up) and the 7 days after it; the likelihood counts only the one-day-ahead predictions of those 7 days. Only fits
             whose 7 days end before a forecast period are used for that period.
  At origin  fit = latest weekly fit ending by the start of day D. Evidence under that fit for the 90 days to D (published loads) and for D+1 (loads of
             the arm: published, or Timestral with extra noise). D+2 has no evidence: the filter predicts through it. Forecast = curve at
             index + predicted state (mean of the Gaussian filter = its median; the curve is monotone in the index, so this is the median price; no
             averaging over the state's uncertainty, which would aim at the mean and sit above the median on a convex curve).
  D+1 noise  under forecast loads: evidence noise + variance of the daily-median index difference between forecast and published loads, measured on
             earlier origins of the same period only (loads, no prices), shrunk towards a prior value of 2 GW^2 with weight 5 origins.
  Refits     by calendar week for every period (the earlier runs refitted every 7 origins, which is every 21 days in winter and summer); the index model
             alone and the three earlier online layers are therefore re-scored on the same weekly fits, so that every comparison is like for like.
15.2 Pass rule, fixed before scoring
  The layer earns its place if, with Timestral loads, it lowers the night MAE AND the first-14-h MAE of the index model alone in all three periods
  (summer, autumn, winter), and does not move the night bias by more than 0.2 ct in any of them. None of the three earlier layers met that (each
  lost in autumn or at 14 h). It is the fourth online layer tried on the same origins; all four are reported.
  Expectation stated in advance: a level filter cannot close the summer gap to the earlier structural curve (1.20 against 0.93 ct at night without any
  online layer); that gap is in the curve. Like for like, the earlier model's own online layer gained 6% / 6% / 27% at night.

15.3 What the checks and the scores showed (state layer on the second index model; all on weekly calendar fits)
  Evidence   usable hours 87-88%; 8-10% at the lower bound (about 90% of those have a price <= 5 EUR/MWh, i.e. midday surplus the curve cannot go below;
             2-3% of night hours), 3-5% at the upper bound (median price 140-170); no hours lost to missing inputs. Curve monotone in the index over
             the whole gas and carbon range. Realised-load term: k3^2 x var(daily median) = 0.5-1.4 GW^2 against 19 GW^2 of evidence variance, and
             itself persistent (0.55-0.59 at one day) -> not injected; the state carries its persistent part. One noise variance.
  Dynamics   daily evidence, in sample: autocorrelation 0.52 0.37 0.30 0.27 0.28 0.25 0.24 0.19 0.13 0.07 at 1-10 days (a one-day memory would give
             0.52 0.27 0.14 0.07 ...). Learned out of sample before each period (winter / summer / autumn): persistence of the fast part 0.85 / 0.69 /
             0.71, its variance 2.0 / 5.6 / 5.4, level variance 0.02 / 0.015 / 0.07 per day, evidence noise 6.6 / 4.9 / 4.1 GW^2; weight on a new
             day's evidence 0.37 / 0.59 / 0.63. Standardised one-day-ahead innovations: sd 1.00, excess kurtosis 0.2-0.5, 1.7-2.3% beyond 2.5 sd,
             none beyond 3.5, autocorrelation within +-0.08 at 1-7 days -> no cap used.
             One-day-ahead error of the evidence [GW]: no layer 4.2 / 3.8 / 4.0, yesterday's value x fitted factor 2.8 / 2.9 / 2.8, filter 2.5 / 2.7 /
             2.6. For the night block alone the filter and yesterday's value are equal (3.35 / 3.59 / 3.49 against 3.34 / 3.61 / 3.50).
  D+1 noise  load-forecast index error of D+1 (daily median): sd 3.7 GW winter, 2.5 summer, 2.2 autumn -> extra noise variance up to 12.7 / 6.1 / 4.8.
  Scores     Timestral loads, MAE ct night / first 14 h, change against the index model alone with block-bootstrap interval:
                                          summer                      autumn                     winter
               index model alone          1.17 / 1.69                 1.68 / 1.98                1.40 / 1.22
               + state layer              0.96 / 1.46  -19% [-28..-8]  1.69 / 1.98  +1% [-5..+7]   1.28 / 1.15  -9% [-18..-1]
               + persistence of D+1       1.08 / 1.57   -8% [-19..+4]  1.71 / 2.01  +2% [-4..+8]   1.28 / 1.13  -9% [-14..-3]
               + hour-specific, price     1.04 / 1.72  -11% [-25..+5]  1.83 / 2.10  +9% [-0..+19]  1.47 / 1.27  +5% [-14..+30]
               + hour-specific, index     0.99 / 1.60  -15% [-29..-1]  1.84 / 2.10 +10% [-1..+21]  1.20 / 1.10 -15% [-30..+3]
               earlier curve              0.93 / 1.49                 1.63 / 1.90                1.19 / 1.10
               earlier curve + its layer  0.87 / 1.28                 1.53 / 1.78                0.87 / 0.91
             Published loads: state layer 0.80 / 1.31 (alone 0.99 / 1.53), 1.53 / 1.78 (1.57 / 1.86), 1.23 / 1.06 (1.32 / 1.10).
  Verdict    By the rule in 15.2 the layer does NOT pass: no gain in autumn (night +0.01, 14 h 0.00). It is the only one of the four layers that
             loses nowhere, and its summer gain is the only one whose interval excludes zero at both horizons. The bias clause of the rule was
             badly written: the summer night bias moved from +0.63 to +0.15, i.e. by more than 0.2 ct but towards zero, which is the purpose.
  Calibration realised evidence of D+2 against the predicted state (Timestral loads): inside the 90% interval 80% (winter, 30 days), 88% (summer),
             89% (autumn); inside the 50% interval 33% / 38% / 46%. Mean error +0.9 GW winter, +1.3 summer, 0.0 autumn: the filter recovers only
             part of a standing miss. Night block mean error +1.7 / +0.5 / -1.1 GW: the day's state does not transfer cleanly to the night.

15.4 What the evidence series itself shows: a trough-day effect (found while checking the state layer)
  Out of sample (each day scored by the latest weekly fit before it), the mean daily evidence by month [GW]:
     2025  Jun +2.0  Jul +6.1  Aug +4.2  Sep +2.6  Oct +2.9  Nov -1.1  Dec -0.2
     2026  Jan +1.0  Feb +1.3  Mar +0.6  Apr +3.0  May +4.4  Jun +3.4  Jul +3.6  Aug +3.8  Sep +0.6  Oct -0.2  Nov +1.8
  The level miss is a summer-half-year effect in BOTH years, not a 2026 drift (the "May-Aug 2026 miss" of sections 9-14 is this). The realised-load
  term is not the cause (mean contribution -0.08 GW).
  By the depth of the day's trough (how far the day's lowest index falls below the curve's surplus point), mean evidence [GW]:
     depth [GW]        0-0.5   0.5-3    3-8    8-15    15+
     before 30 Mar 26  -0.73   +1.63   +3.02   +3.93   +3.53     (301 days; used for the choice)
     from 30 Mar 26    +0.91   +0.47   +2.27   +2.94   +3.77     (223 days; not used for the choice)
  By hours of model surplus in the day: none +0.65 / +1.18; 1-3 h +3.6 / +3.0; 4-6 h +4.0 / +4.0; 7-9 h +4.8 / +3.4.
  The effect is uniform over the day's usable hours (trough days: +3..+5 GW at night, morning and evening; other days about 0 at night) and belongs
  to the day itself: regression on today's saturated depth and the previous week's: +3.9 x today +1.6 x previous week before 30 March, +3.1 and
  +0.6 after (night: +5.1 / -1.1 and +4.4 / -1.7).
  Third index model (exp/ocxt.py), specification fixed before scoring: second version + x_h -> x_h + kc * sigmoid((qc - min over the day of x) / wc),
  kc ~ LogNormal(log 2, 0.8) GW, qc ~ Normal(8, 6) GW, wc ~ LogNormal(log 3, 0.5) GW, learned with everything else; needs only the day's load
  profile, so it exists for D+2. Reading (not tested): plant that cannot ride through the midday trough stays off, so the rest of the day clears
  higher up the offer curve.
  Expectation before scoring: most of the Apr-Aug evidence disappears; summer night MAE of the model alone falls from 1.17 towards the state
  layer's 0.96; autumn and winter change little. The same pass rule applies to the term as to a layer (lower night and 14 h MAE in all three
  periods). Summer and autumn are out of sample for the choice; winter is inside the data the choice was made on.
  Simulated-data check of the filter and its learner (exp/ocx_state.py sim; 8 replications, 43 weekly segments, 4% of days missing at random plus
  six gaps of 2-5 days): persistence true 0.70, recovered 0.70 (0.58..0.76); fast variance 5.0 -> 4.4 (3.2..7.3); level variance 0.03 -> 0.03
  (0.01..0.08); noise 5.0 -> 5.6 (4.1..8.0). One-day-ahead state intervals cover 49% / 90% (nominal 50 / 90); on days with no evidence 46% / 89%.
  Figure: out/state_fig.png.

15.5 Third index model (trough-day term) scored: the expectation of 15.4 FAILED; the term is withdrawn
  Timestral loads, weekly calendar fits, MAE ct night / first 14 h:
                                  summer        autumn        winter
     second model alone           1.17 / 1.69   1.68 / 1.98   1.40 / 1.22
     trough model alone           1.13 / 1.66   1.74 / 2.04   1.36 / 1.20
     second model + state layer   0.96 / 1.46   1.69 / 1.98   1.28 / 1.15
     trough model + state layer   0.96 / 1.47   1.71 / 2.00   1.16 / 1.06
  Expected: summer night towards 0.96 and most of the Apr-Aug evidence gone. Found: summer 1.13, autumn worse, and the out-of-sample evidence still
  +2.6..+3.8 GW in May-Aug 2026 (second model +3.3..+4.4) and larger in Sep-Oct 2025 (+4.0, +4.7 against +2.6, +2.9); sd of the monthly means
  1.90 -> 1.70 GW. In-sample MAE 1.27 -> 1.22 ct. The term's parameters wander across the weekly fits (kc 5.5..8.9 GW, qc 10.6..22.8 GW, and the
  curve's surplus point qa 19 -> 2.5 GW): it is absorbed as a general offset together with the curve's location, not as a contrast between trough
  days and other days. A single frozen fit (2026-03-30, applied to Apr-Aug 2026) did gain: night MAE 1.39 -> 1.16, evidence +3.5..+8.3 -> +2.1..+6.3 GW,
  growing with the distance from the fit date in both versions; with weekly refits most of that gain is already there without the term.
  Censoring: treating prices <= 0 as censored in the likelihood (all the curve is told is "that low") had no effect in the one fit that ran
  (frozen fit 2026-03-30, bound at 0: monthly evidence within 0.2 GW, night MAE 1.39 against 1.38 without the trough term and 1.16 against 1.16
  with it). The second fit (2025-06-02) returned NaN under censoring, so this rests on one fit and one bound.
  Caveat on the evidence statistic: hours at the lower bound are dropped, and they are concentrated on trough days, so the median of the remaining
  hours is pushed up on exactly those days. Selection-free version (night hours 0-7, all hours counted, price error actual - model, ct) by trough
  depth 0-0.5 / 0.5-3 / 3-8 / 8-15 / 15+ GW: before 30 Mar 2026  -0.02 +0.38 +1.00 +0.96 +0.50;  from 30 Mar 2026  -0.02 -0.28 +0.07 +0.22 +0.70.
  Within one season the day effect is not consistent: Jun-Sep 2025 night error +0.51 on trough days against +0.72 on other days; Apr-Aug 2026
  +0.59 against +0.29; Nov 2025-Mar 2026 +1.29 (30 days) against +0.14; Sep-Nov 2026 +0.38 against -0.45.
  What stands: out of sample (1-7 days after each weekly fit) the second model's night price error is positive in 15 of 18 months from June 2025
  (+0.2..+0.8 ct; exceptions Nov 2025, Sep and Oct 2026). The level miss is therefore not a 2026 event. What does not stand: that a day-level
  trough shift is the mechanism (see the corrections in 15.7). Not tested: a sharper low end of the curve (price errors on
  moderate-trough days are largest in the hours nearest the day's low, +1.5..+3.5 ct), an hour-block state (night against the rest of the day).

15.6 Control without dynamics, reading rule written BEFORE running it
  The evidence is positive already in sample (+1.48 GW over the 90 days before a fit, +1.87 in its last 14 days; +1.3..+2.8 out of sample), and the
  state layer differs from the persistence layer only in summer, where it moves the night bias from +0.63 to +0.15 ct. Control arm: shift = plain
  mean of the 90 days of evidence to D under the current fit, no filter, same for every forecast hour.
  Reading: if this arm gets two thirds or more of the state layer's summer gain (night MAE 1.03 or lower against 1.17 alone and 0.96 with the
  state), the layer's gain is recalibration of a curve whose median residual in the usable hours is not zero, not tracking of a moving level.
  Result (Timestral loads, MAE ct night / first 14 h; change against the index model alone):
                                    summer                        autumn                       winter
     index model alone              1.17 / 1.69                   1.68 / 1.98                  1.40 / 1.22
     + 90-day mean, no dynamics     1.09 / 1.59   -7% [-13..-0]   1.76 / 1.98   +5% [-2..+13]  1.40 / 1.23   0% [-2..+2]
     + state layer                  0.96 / 1.46  -19% [-28..-8]   1.69 / 1.98   +1% [-5..+7]   1.28 / 1.15  -9% [-18..-1]
     state layer against the mean   -12% [-19..-5]                -4% [-13..+4]                -9% [-17..-1]
  The control gets about 40% of the summer gain (0.08 of 0.21 ct), below the two-thirds line, nothing in winter, and it is worse in autumn (night
  bias -0.13 -> -0.68). Reading: in summer part of the layer's gain is recalibration of a curve whose median residual is not zero; most of it,
  and all of the winter gain, comes from the dynamics.

15.7 Corrections to 15.4 and 15.5 (after review)
  - WITHDRAWN: "the level miss is a summer-half-year effect in both years". The seasonality is in GW of index shift, where the dropped surplus
    hours inflate the evidence on trough days and where the curve is flat (more GW per ct). In price terms and without selection the night error is
    +0.38 / +0.54 / +0.77 ct in Dec-Feb against +0.34..+0.64 ct in Apr-Aug 2026. What the series supports is a positive out-of-sample night
    error in most months, not a summer effect. (An earlier version of this line said the 2025 fits had data only from July 2024. Wrong: the
    data start 2024-03-01; the fit of 2025-06-02 had 438 days, and every fit from 2025-10-06 has the full 540.)
  - WITHDRAWN: "dose-response in both samples". Selection-free, after 30 March 2026 only the deepest bin shows it (-0.02 -0.28 +0.07 +0.22 +0.70).
  - WITHDRAWN: "that makes it a structural term rather than a state". Fitted as a term it failed its stated expectation (15.5).
  - DROPPED from "what stands": "zero on days without a trough in both samples"; the pooled figure mixes +0.72 (Jun-Sep 2025) with -0.45 (Sep-Nov 2026).
  - Not to be leaned on: trough model + state layer in winter (1.16 / 1.06). Winter is inside the data the term was chosen on, and the term failed
    in the two periods that are out of sample for it.
  - Not run: the block-data imputation check (fresh owner states from the fleet total and last known shares, against 1.22 / 1.74, 1.72 / 1.98, 1.46 / 1.27).


16. WHERE THE INSTABILITY IS, AND WHAT A TIGHT PRIOR CAN DO ABOUT IT (user: "instability issues should get tight priored away?")
==========================================================================================================================
16.1 Three checks (second index model, 76 weekly fits)
  a. Parameters across fits: heat threshold 14..21 C (prior 13 +- 2.5; median sits +2.4 prior sd out), neighbours' weights CH 0.34..1.75 (+2.8 sd),
     BE 0.48..1.06 (+2.0), NL (+1.7); curve knots qa -3..11, qw 6..24, qb 43..50 GW moving together (qa~qw 0.93, qw~qb 0.91, qa~kh -0.80).
  b. Optimiser: the cached fits (2500 steps) have not arrived. Same data, fit 2026-03-30: three seeds at 2500 steps give qb 46 / 40 / 49 GW and
     log posterior -5487 / -5494 / -5486; 8000 and 30000 steps both give -5482.26 with qb 60, qw 29, c0 15.3. The step count has acted as an
     unstated prior.
  c. In function space (1252 panel hours Jul 2025 - Jun 2026, their own loads and availability):
     - week to week the prediction for the same hour and the same costs moves by 0.03 ct on average (95th percentile 0.11): the weekly jitter of the
       parameters is NOT a jitter of the function. A prior that removes it changes no forecast.
     - converged against unconverged (same data): 0.07..0.12 ct apart at gas 35, but -0.26 ct at gas 60 and -0.6 ct at gas 90 in the 15-50 GW
       range (>= 50 GW: 0.5 / 0.7 / 1.1 ct). The function is pinned where the window has data and loose where costs have to be extrapolated.
     - across the 58 full-window fits (Oct 2025 - Nov 2026) the response to cost ROTATES: implied pass-through of gas-fired cost L in the 15-35 GW
       range 0.57 0.51 0.51 0.50 0.57 0.67 0.73 0.81 0.82 (every 7th fit), in 35-50 GW 1.13 -> 1.21, >= 50 GW 1.6 throughout. Gas in the window was
       30..50 until February 2026 and reached 92 by November. Evaluated at each fit's own current costs and in units of L the function is flat
       (15-35 GW 0.87 0.91 0.98 0.89 0.81 0.86 0.88 0.89 0.87; 35-50 GW 1.16..1.23; >= 50 GW 1.57..1.67). So it is not a level drift and a time
       term would be the wrong fix: the fits learned an affine response (a solid-fuel cost that does not move with gas, plus a gas slope of
       about 0.5) from a window in which gas hardly moved, and re-learn it as gas rises.
16.2 Outside knowledge on the pass-through (exp/passthru_hist.py; slope of price on L, least absolute deviations over days, by residual load net of nuclear)
                              0-15   15-25   25-35   35-50   50+ GW
     2021-2023 (gas 18..227)  0.20   0.51    0.81    1.08    1.39     intercepts +15 +17 +8 +6 +5 EUR/MWh
     Mar 2024 - Feb 2026      0.12   0.50    0.66    0.74    0.79     intercepts -8 +21 +28 +40 +76 (gas 28..55: weakly identified)
     Mar - Nov 2026           0.94   0.89    1.03    1.23     -       intercepts -82 -11 +1 +1 (gas 35..95)
     price / L (median)       2021-23: 0.31 0.65 0.89 1.14 1.44 | 2024-Feb 2026: 0.03 0.72 0.97 1.17 1.56 | Mar-Nov 2026: 0.36 0.81 1.04 1.24
  Above 25 GW the price is close to proportional to L whenever gas moves enough to tell; the window of the pre-2026 fits could not tell.
16.3 Arms, fixed BEFORE fitting (all at 8000 steps, weekly fits from 2025-11-24; models scored alone, no online layer)
  C   current priors, converged.
  P1  tight priors where there is outside knowledge and the data pull a parameter away as a proxy: heat threshold N(13, 1) C (heat-led must-run
      finding 12-14 C), heat slope logN(0.4, 0.3) GW/C, heat term centred at 10 C so that it carries no offset; neighbours' weights
      logN(0.3, 0.25); curve knots as one location qa and two differences (qw - qa ~ logN(10, 0.5), qb - qw ~ logN(22, 0.4)) instead of three
      free positions.
  P2  P1 + a prior on the FUNCTION: pass-through of gas-fired cost, w(x) b(x), at index 20 / 30 / 42.5 / 60 GW ~ N(0.51 / 0.81 / 1.08 / 1.39, 0.10),
      the 2021-2023 values. Nothing from 2026 enters.
  Expectations: P1: parameters stable by construction; no forecast gain, possibly a loss (the freed parameters were proxies); report how far the
  heat threshold and the CH weight still sit from their priors and where the misfit went. P2: pass-through at 15-35 GW of the pre-2026 fits rises
  from 0.5 towards 0.65-0.8 and stops rotating; night and 14 h MAE fall in summer (gas 35 -> 50 in spring 2026) and autumn (gas 60 -> 90), winter
  (gas flat 30..38) within +-2%. Rule: P2 passes if it is lower than C at night and at 14 h in summer and autumn and within +-2% in winter.
16.4 First look at P1 / P2 on two dates, and a fourth arm declared before any forecast is scored
  Fit 2026-03-30 (gas 30..54 in the window): pass-through at 20 / 30 / 42.5 / 60 GW  P1 0.23 0.46 0.85 1.33, P2 0.28 0.54 0.93 1.36 (2021-2023:
  0.51 0.81 1.08 1.39). The P2 prior (sd 0.10) hardly binds: the window's own data prefer a low pass-through. Fit 2026-10-19 (gas 30..75):
  0.78 0.99 1.16 1.52 in both arms, with the curve reshaped (gas pricing nearly everywhere above 20 GW: qw 36 -> 10 GW, c0 17 -> 7).
  Tight priors on the proxies work as intended for the neighbours (CH weight 0.33..0.38, +0.4..+0.9 prior sd; in-sample MAE 1.26 -> 1.28 ct), but the
  heat threshold is still pulled: 14.1 C in March, 16.8 C in October (+3.8 sd of a N(13, 1) prior).
  Why P2 does not bind: gas moves slowly, so the window holds perhaps 18 independent pieces of information about the response to cost, while the
  likelihood (tempered by 0.1) counts about 1300. A prior on the response to a slow driver has to be tighter by roughly the square root of that
  ratio (about 8) to carry the weight its stated sd implies.
  The more transferable quantity is not the pass-through but the ELASTICITY (pass-through / ratio = gas-priced share of the price): pooled 2021-2023
  0.78 / 0.91 / 0.95 / 0.97 at 15-25 / 25-35 / 35-50 / 50+ GW; Mar-Nov 2026 1.1 / 0.99 / 0.99; whereas the pass-through itself differs between the
  two regimes (0.81 against 1.03 at 25-35 GW). Within single years it is poorly identified (2021 0.74, 2022 1.34, 2023 0.53 at 25-35 GW), which is
  the same confounding the current window suffers from.
  P3  P1 + prior on the elasticity at reference costs (gas 35, carbon 75) at index 20 / 30 / 42.5 / 60 GW ~ N(0.78 / 0.91 / 0.95 / 0.97,
      0.05 / 0.02 / 0.02 / 0.02): the pooled 2021-2023 values, with the sd divided by about 8 from an honest 0.15-0.4 for the reason above.
  Expectation for P3: as stated for P2 in 16.3 (gain in summer and autumn, winter within +-2%); same rule. P2 is scored as registered.
16.5 Frozen-fit demonstration, expectation written BEFORE running
  Weekly refitting may be hiding what the priors do. One fit dated 2026-03-30 (gas 30..54 in its window), applied unchanged to every later origin of
  summer and autumn 2026 (gas rising to 90), arms C, P1, P3. Expectation: P3 clearly better than C in autumn, because C's pass-through at 30 GW was
  0.65 in March against the 1.0-1.1 it learned later, while P3 already had 0.85. This is a demonstration of what the prior buys when the model
  cannot follow costs by refitting; it is not part of the pass rule.
16.6 Results
  Stability across the 51 weekly fits (Nov 2025 - Nov 2026), range and largest week-to-week jump:
                                  C (current priors, converged)   P1 (tight proxies, knot differences)   P3 (P1 + elasticity prior)
     pass-through at 30 GW        0.56..1.11   (0.06)             0.33..0.99   (0.10)                    0.85..0.96   (0.01)
     heat threshold, C            15.3..20.3   (2.2)              13.0..16.9   (0.6)                     13.2..17.0   (0.6)
     weight of Swiss load         0.38..1.59   (0.30)             0.32..0.42   (0.03)                    0.32..0.44   (0.03)
     knot qw, GW                  -1.3..33.3   (2.7)              10.0..43.5   (3.8)                     13.2..19.9   (1.3)
     solid availability weight    0.29..1.03   (0.22)             0.24..0.96   (0.20)                    0.36..0.97   (0.18)
     in-sample MAE, ct            1.26 at 2026-03-30              1.24..1.39 (1.28 at 2026-03-30)        1.24..1.42 (1.32 at 2026-03-30)
  The tight priors do what they are for: the Swiss weight and the cost response stop moving. The heat threshold is still pulled up to 17 C by the
  autumn fits (+3.9 sd of N(13, 1)): something seasonal is still being asked of it. The solid-availability weight still triples; it has no outside
  prior yet.
  Forecasts, models alone, Timestral loads, weekly refits, MAE ct night / first 14 h, change against C with block-bootstrap interval:
                                  summer                       autumn                       winter
     C   current, converged       1.16 / 1.68                  1.66 / 1.95                  1.43 / 1.24
         same at 2500 steps       1.17 / 1.69   +1% [-1..+3]   1.68 / 1.98   +1% [-3..+5]   1.40 / 1.22   -2% [-6..+1]
     P1                           1.19 / 1.69   +3% [-0..+6]   1.72 / 1.99   +3% [+2..+5]   1.45 / 1.26   +1% [-1..+5]
     P2                           1.18 / 1.68   +2% [-2..+6]   1.72 / 2.00   +3% [+2..+5]   1.41 / 1.23   -1% [-5..+1]
     P3                           1.14 / 1.64   -2% [-8..+4]   1.71 / 2.01   +3% [-0..+6]   1.46 / 1.30   +2% [-7..+11]
  By the rule of 16.3 neither P2 nor P3 passes (no gain in autumn; P3 also +5% at 14 h in winter, night bias +0.57 -> +0.95). The expectation for
  P1 (no gain, possibly a small loss) held; the expectation for P2 / P3 (gain in summer and autumn) did not. Convergence makes no difference to
  the weekly-refit scores.
  Frozen fit of 2026-03-30 applied unchanged to all later origins (16.5), MAE ct night / first 14 h:
                                  summer (gas 35 -> 60)            autumn (gas 76 -> 99)
     C frozen                     1.80 / 2.22                      2.52 / 3.03   night bias +2.10
     P1 frozen                    1.96 / 2.32   +9% [+5..+13]      2.86 / 3.36   +13% [+9..+17]
     P3 frozen                    1.37 / 1.85  -24% [-30..-17]     1.77 / 2.25   -30% [-40..-18]   night bias +0.51
     C / P3 refitted weekly       1.16 / 1.14                      1.66 / 1.71
  The expectation of 16.5 held. With the elasticity pinned from 2021-2023, a fit that never saw gas above 54 is, AT NIGHT, within 4-7% of the
  weekly refits in autumn, seven months later with gas at 76-99 (1.77 against 1.66 / 1.71). At 14 h it is not: 2.25 against 1.95 / 2.01, bias
  +1.24 ct. Without the prior the same fit is 50% worse at night (converged) or 30% worse (2500 steps as cached; see 16.7). This is consistent
  with the "level problem" of sections 9-14 (data +1.67 EUR per EUR of gas, models 1.0-1.2): P3 has 1.85 x 0.85 = 1.57 at 30 GW from the prior.
  That is one index point, and exp/passthru.py was not run on P3.
  P3 is not clean of 2026: its values are from 2021-2023, but elasticity as the quantity to pin and the tightness were chosen after seeing the
  2026 slopes and the October fits; the division of the sd by about 8 is a judgment, not a measurement.
  Reading. (i) Week-to-week parameter jitter was not costing anything: the function was already stable. (ii) The instability that mattered is the
  response to a slow driver (gas) that the window barely identifies; a tight prior on the right quantity (the elasticity, not the knots) removes it.
  (iii) Under weekly refitting the refit was already doing that job with a lag the scores hardly see, so the forecast table does not move.
  (iv) What the weekly-refit scores still miss (night bias +0.5..+0.6 ct in summer and winter, the gap to the earlier curve and to the trees) is
  therefore not a parameter-stability problem.
16.7 Second freeze date, expectation written BEFORE running (after review)
  P3's form (elasticity) and its tightness were chosen after looking at the fit of 2026-03-30, which is also the date frozen in 16.5; the frozen
  result could be tuned to that date. Second date: 2026-01-05 (gas 30..50 in its window), arms C and P3, plus the fit at 2500 steps as cached
  (converged C sits 0.6 ct lower at gas 90, so it is the weakest baseline). Expectation: P3 frozen in January beats both baselines in summer and in
  autumn by a similar margin as in 16.5. If it does not, the March result does not carry.
  Result, MAE ct night / first 14 h, Timestral loads:
                                  frozen 2026-01-05                              frozen 2026-03-30
                                  summer        autumn                           summer        autumn
     C converged                  1.87 / 2.30   2.74 / 3.26                      1.80 / 2.22   2.52 / 3.03
     C at 2500 steps (as cached)  1.72 / 2.19   2.40 / 2.96                      1.63 / 2.09   2.19 / 2.73
     P3                           1.44 / 1.94   1.87 / 2.39                      1.37 / 1.85   1.77 / 2.25
     P3 against C at 2500 steps   -17% [-22..-10]  -22% [-31..-12] (night)       -16% [-21..-10]  -19% [-28..-9] (night)
     C refitted weekly            1.16 / 1.68   1.66 / 1.95
  The expectation held at the second date and against the stronger baseline. The unconverged fit is the better frozen baseline by 8-13%: stopping
  the optimiser early had been acting as a prior towards a higher pass-through.


17. BACK TO THE CANDIDATE: WHERE IT LOSES, AND A BLEND WEIGHT THAT DEPENDS ON THE LOAD RANGE
============================================================================================
(Repository: main fast-forwarded to 2d942f6 on the user's request, the tree models with residual-load forecast and optional Timestral blend,
 installable with the Proxmox script. Nothing of the structural work is in the repository.)
17.1 Why nothing of sections 15-16 reached the candidate: all of it was done on the index model, which is 15-45% behind the candidate's curve on its
  own. The candidate is (structural curve of the frozen spec, runs hs2 / ha / hw2, with its online layer dyn2 x+xh) averaged 50/50 with Timestral's
  price forecast. Verified: that average reproduces out/cand_<period>.npz exactly (0.79 / 1.28, 1.31 / 1.63, 0.67 / 0.88).
17.2 Where the structural curve loses to the trees (exp/gap_diag.py, exp/low_look.py; published loads, night; runs ys / ya / yw):
  all of the gap is at effective residual load x <= 15 GW: autumn 2.21 against 1.72 ct (186% of the gap), winter 1.58 against 0.69 (110%), summer
  1.45 against 1.22; above 45 GW the structural curve is better (autumn 1.15 / 1.34, summer 0.69 / 0.87). These are whole nights mispriced (7 nights
  in winter, 16 in autumn, 10 in summer), not shape errors within a night. In winter the curve alone is 2.4 ct too low there (0.9 with its layer).
  Already examined on 2026-11-16 (out/mechanisms_summary.txt): the trees get those nights through a date feature and predicted neighbour prices;
  two-zone equilibrium, segment trend and information-based forgetting were tried on the curve and not adopted. Not repeated.
  The index model is worse there, not better (winter x <= 15: 3.47 ct, bias -3.4).
17.3 Live arm, by the structural model's own x (exp/blend_look.py, runs ys / ya / yw): Timestral's price is the better of the two at 5-15 GW in all
  three periods (night: 3.05 / 2.02 / 0.77 against 3.62 / 3.04 / 1.55), the structural curve above 45 GW (0.75 / 1.39 / 0.93 against 0.93 / 1.48 /
  1.01; first 14 h 0.87 / 1.48 / 0.92 against 1.04 / 1.74 / 1.48). Best weight per range in hindsight (upper bound): night 0.81 -> 0.79, 1.30 -> 1.25,
  0.71 -> 0.64; first 14 h 1.32 -> 1.26, 1.62 -> 1.61, 0.88 -> 0.81.
17.4 Test, fixed BEFORE running: the canonical candidate's two components (hs2 / ha / hw2 + layer; Timestral price), weight on the structural curve
  w(x) = sigmoid(a + b (x - 30) / 15), x = the structural model's effective residual load from Timestral loads (known at the origin).
  a, b chosen at each origin from all forecasts whose outcome is known by then (issued at least 2 days earlier, all periods in time order), by
  minimising mean absolute error over the first 14 h plus 0.01 ct x (a^2 + b^2), which pulls towards the plain average; fewer than 15 earlier
  forecasts -> plain average. Rule: passes if lower than the plain average at night AND at 14 h in summer and in autumn, and not more than 1% worse
  in winter (where there is almost no history to learn from). Expectation: half of the hindsight gain or less; b > 0.
17.5 Result of 17.4: NOT adopted. Walk-forward, MAE ct night / first 14 h: winter 0.68 / 0.88 (plain average 0.67 / 0.88), summer 0.81 / 1.26
  (0.79 / 1.28), autumn 1.37 / 1.67 (1.31 / 1.63; night +4% [+0..+8]). On the canonical runs the pooled best weight function has no slope at all
  (a +0.25, b 0.00, weight 0.56 everywhere). (An earlier version of this line said the per-range pattern of 17.3 does not carry over to the
  canonical runs. Wrong, see 17.11: it is there at night; this test pooled the first 14 h.) The plain average stands. For reference, the candidate against the trees alone at night: winter 0.67 / 0.72, summer
  0.79 / 0.89 (+13% [+1..+31] for the trees), autumn 1.31 / 1.39; at 14 h 0.88 / 0.82, 1.28 / 1.31, 1.63 / 1.65.
17.6 Supply side ("offer curve") terms: are they worth anything in a forecast? Expectation written BEFORE running.
  The offer-curve work (sections 9-14) ended in the index model because the German price follows the regional residual load more tightly than any
  German generation quantity. What it left behind as supply-side information are three shifts of the index: available lignite + hard coal (14-day
  maximum, known to D-3), available gas (last known day's maximum) and the neighbours' available nuclear (14-day maximum). They passed the screening
  before 2026-03-31, but their value in the forecasts was never isolated, and the candidate's curve has none of them.
  Ablation: arm C (index model, current priors, converged) with the three weights fixed at zero, weekly refits, models alone.
  Expectation: if the supply side carries forecast value, the ablated model is worse at night in all three periods by a few percent. If it is
  not worse, the terms are not worth porting into the candidate's curve. If it is, port them as shifts of x and re-score the candidate.
17.7 Result of the ablation (17.6): the supply side carries forecast value.
  Index model, weekly refits, Timestral loads, MAE ct night / first 14 h, with -> without the three supply-side terms:
     summer 1.16 / 1.68 -> 1.32 / 1.77  (+14% [+4..+24] at night);  autumn 1.66 / 1.95 -> 1.80 / 2.01  (+8% [+3..+15]);
     winter 1.43 / 1.24 -> 1.52 / 1.34  (+6% [-3..+16]).   Published loads: +15% [+5..+27], +10% [+3..+18], +3% [-7..+16] at night.
  With the state layer on top about half remains: +7% [+2..+12], +3% [-0..+7], +3% [-2..+9]. The states are slow (day-to-day sd 0.5 GW for solid
  fuel and nuclear against 2.5 and 4.7 GW over the period), so an online level layer absorbs part of what they say.
  Two other checks on the candidate's curve itself, both negative: (i) its fitted gas response looks jumpy from fit to fit (0.55..1.25 at 30 GW in
  winter), but forecasts of the same hour from consecutive daily fits differ by 0.44 ct with identical inputs (0.19 ct of that is the day's gas
  move), and averaging the two is no better than the newer one (1.73 against 1.70), so fit noise is not costing accuracy; (ii) convergence was not
  tested on it.
17.8 Port into the candidate's curve, fixed BEFORE running
  exp/smodel.py, option AV=1: x -> x - ka . (availability - its training mean), three states as in the index model (lignite + hard coal 14-day
  maximum, gas last known day's maximum, neighbours' nuclear 14-day maximum; all built from days <= d-3, so the value at the first forecast hour
  is known at the origin and is used for the whole horizon), ka ~ logN(0.5, 0.5), logN(0.3, 0.7), logN(0.3, 0.5) as in the index model. Everything
  else as in the candidate's runs (DRIFT=s BTOP=45; same arms, same online layer dyn2 x+xh; 50/50 with the same Timestral price forecasts).
  With AV=0 the edited script must reproduce a stored fit exactly.
  Expectation: structural + layer 3-7% better at night; candidate 1-3% better. Rule (as on 2026-11-16): adopted only if the candidate is better
  at night in all three periods and not more than 1% worse at 14 h in any.
17.9 Result of the port (17.8): NOT adopted; the candidate does not move.
  With AV=0 the edited smodel.py reproduces the stored fit of 2026-09-18 exactly (max difference 0.0). Runs vw / vs / va (AV=1), same layer, same
  Timestral price. MAE ct night / first 14 h, Timestral loads, as it is -> with the supply side:
                           winter                       summer                       autumn
     curve only            1.41 / 1.22 -> 1.45 / 1.25   0.98 / 1.55 -> 0.94 / 1.53   1.69 / 1.98 -> 1.68 / 1.97
     curve + its layer     0.86 / 0.93 -> 0.87 / 0.94   0.89 / 1.26 -> 0.89 / 1.26   1.59 / 1.85 -> 1.58 / 1.85
     candidate             0.67 / 0.88 -> 0.68 / 0.87   0.79 / 1.28 -> 0.78 / 1.28   1.31 / 1.63 -> 1.31 / 1.63
                           +1% [-1..+3]                 -0% [-1..+2]                 -0% [-1..+1]      (night, against the candidate)
  The expectation (structural part 3-7% better) did not hold. Fitted weights are small and sit against the lower side of their priors: solid fuel
  0.27 / 0.41 / 0.27, gas 0.05, neighbours' nuclear 0.07-0.11 (index model: 0.73, 0.11, 0.37), i.e. 2-3 prior sd below centre under priors that
  allow only positive weights; the in-sample error did not improve either (first autumn fit: 12.6 against 12.4 EUR/MWh, last 30 days 17.9 against
  16.7). The terms did not move the candidate; WHY is not established. Candidates for the reason, none tested: the curve's weekly drift of the
  price scale, its Europe-wide weather terms and its online layer may already carry what these slow states say; the positive-only priors may be
  forcing weights the data do not want; the candidate's fits were not checked for convergence (4000 steps).
  The ablation result in the index model stands (6-14% at night there).
  Figures: out/gas_fig.png (gas response fit by fit; frozen fits), out/cand_fig.png (errors by load range; supply side).
17.10 Standing after this session: candidate unchanged at 0.79 / 1.28 (summer), 1.31 / 1.63 (autumn), 0.67 / 0.88 (winter). Against the trees alone
  its night MAE is lower in all three periods (trees 0.89, 1.39, 0.72), but only summer's interval excludes zero (+13% [+1..+31] for the trees;
  winter +7% [-9..+24], autumn +6% [-2..+15]); at 14 h it is behind in winter (0.88 against 0.82). Against trees + Timestral
  price it is level (0.80 / 1.29, 1.29 / 1.58, 0.68 / 0.85). Tested today on the candidate and not adopted: load-range blend weight, supply-side
  shifts. Tested on the index model only: state filter (gain there, model still behind), trough term (failed), tight priors (stable parameters,
  no score gain under weekly refits; gas-share prior worth 16-22% for frozen fits).
17.11 Second attempt at the load-range blend weight, night only; rule written BEFORE running (after review)
  The reason given in 17.5 was wrong. The canonical runs do show the pattern at night (out/cand_fig.png, top row: autumn 5-15 GW about 3.0 ct for
  the structural curve against 1.9 for Timestral's price; winter below 15 GW 1.2-1.9 against 0.8-1.2). What 17.4 tested was one weight function
  learned on the first 14 h, whose low-load hours are mostly midday, where the structural curve is level or better.
  Second attempt: the same procedure with the weight learned on and applied to the night hours only (first 8 h); plain average for the other hours.
  Rule: adopted only if the night MAE is lower than the plain average in summer and in autumn and not more than 1% worse in winter. This is a
  second try on the same forecasts and is labelled as such.
  Result of 17.11: NOT adopted. Night MAE, plain average -> night-only weight: winter 0.67 -> 0.67 (-1% [-7..+4]), summer 0.79 -> 0.80
  (+2% [-1..+4]), autumn 1.31 -> 1.29 (-1% [-3..+0]). The learned weight is nearly flat (0.36 / 0.44 / 0.52 on the structural curve at 10 / 30 /
  50 GW; the same in hindsight over all 146 forecasts). The pattern is real but small against the noise of 7-16 low-load nights per period.
  Not run in this session: the block-data imputation check (15.7), convergence of the candidate's fits, the gas-share prior on the candidate's
  curve, the state filter's intervals on the candidate. The new-container install path was not exercised from here (no Proxmox host); the URLs
  it depends on resolve (the community-scripts core build.func and both scripts on main).


18. GAS-SHARE PRIOR ON THE CANDIDATE'S CURVE (user: "go for the gas-share prior on the candidate")
===============================================================================================
18.1 Specification, fixed BEFORE any fit
  The candidate's curve is price = [b(x) + gas * h(x)] * weekly scale drift; it has no carbon term, so b carries carbon. The values of 16.4 are
  shares of gas-fired cost L = 1.85 gas + 0.37 carbon, so they are converted, not copied:
      target for  g h(x) / (b(x) + g h(x))  at x = 20 / 30 / 42.5 / 60 GW  =  (0.78 / 0.91 / 0.95 / 0.97)  x  1.85 g / (1.85 g + 0.37 c),
  g and c the mean gas and carbon price of each fit's own training window; sd (0.05 / 0.02 / 0.02 / 0.02) scaled by the same factor. Nothing else
  changes (option GS=1 in exp/smodel.py; DRIFT=s BTOP=45 as in the candidate). The tightness is the judgment made on the index model and is not
  retuned here. Check of the conversion: at gas 41, carbon 75 the targets are 0.57 / 0.67 / 0.70 / 0.71; the stored fit of 2026-09-18 has
  0.45 / 0.43 / 0.43 / 0.57.
  Frozen mode (option FREEZE=NAME/TAG): the parameters of one fit, with that fit's weather normalisation and reference gas, are used unchanged at
  every later origin; no fitting; the error history that feeds the online layer is computed with the frozen curve (no weekly scale drift, which
  belongs to the freeze window). Baseline and prior arm go through the same code path.
18.2 Order of work and rules, fixed BEFORE any score
  a. Reachability: fit 2026-01-06, 2026-03-31 and 2026-09-18 with the prior, at 4000 steps (as the candidate) and 12000; report the achieved share
     in prior sd, b and h at the four points, in-sample error. The target needs b nearly flat at 40-60 EUR/MWh from 20 to 60 GW (now 55 -> 85). If
     the prior does not bind, that is reported; it is not tightened.
  b. Frozen test (the purpose of the prior). Freeze dates 2026-01-06 (headline; the March date is the one the prior was shaped on) and 2026-03-31;
     applied to summer and autumn; both step counts. Expectation is about the CURVE ALONE (isolates the prior): with the prior it is better at
     night than the frozen baseline in autumn at both dates and in summer for the January date, by 10-25%. With the online layer on top (what an
     installed app would see) the gap is expected smaller but of the same sign. Rule: passes if better than the frozen baseline at both freeze
     dates, curve alone, at night, in autumn.
  c. Under refits at every origin (the candidate as scored): rule is NO HARM, the candidate within +1% at night and at 14 h in each period.
  d. Stability: the range of the gas response at 30 GW across origins (0.55..1.25 now) and the 0.44 ct difference between forecasts of the same
     hour from consecutive daily fits should both fall.
  The prior goes into the candidate's curve if b passes and c shows no harm.
18.3 Reachability (18.2 a): the prior binds, is reachable, and costs nothing in sample
  Gas share at 20 / 30 / 42.5 / 60 GW at the window's mean gas; target 0.57 / 0.66 / 0.69 / 0.71 (Jan, Mar) and 0.57 / 0.67 / 0.70 / 0.71 (Sep):
                         as the candidate, 4000 steps        as the candidate, 12000 steps       with the prior, 4000        with the prior, 12000
     fit 2026-01-06      0.59 0.65 0.71 0.77  MAE 1.244      0.42 0.53 0.61 0.69  MAE 1.233      0.58 0.65 0.68 0.74  1.243  0.57 0.65 0.68 0.73  1.233
     fit 2026-03-31      0.35 0.48 0.56 0.65  MAE 1.212      0.33 0.46 0.55 0.64  MAE 1.204      0.56 0.64 0.68 0.73  1.218  0.55 0.64 0.68 0.73  1.205
     fit 2026-09-18      0.45 0.43 0.43 0.57  MAE 1.310      0.46 0.44 0.44 0.54  MAE 1.252      0.64 0.65 0.66 0.73  1.311  0.60 0.66 0.67 0.72  1.253
  With the prior the share is within 0-2 prior sd of its target; without it the baseline is 3-18 sd away in March and September. In-sample MAE is
  the same with and without the prior at equal steps: the window does not identify the split between b and gas x h, and the prior chooses it.
  Without the prior the split depends on the step count (January: h at 30 GW 1.76 at 4000 steps, 1.36 at 12000), so the 4000-step baseline of
  January sits near the target by accident; both step counts are reported for the frozen test.
  Side finding: the candidate's fits are not converged at 4000 steps. Loss 6897 -> 6887, 6848 -> 6844, 6943 -> 6912; in-sample MAE 1.244 -> 1.233,
  1.212 -> 1.204, 1.310 -> 1.252 ct (last 30 days of the September fit 1.64 -> 1.50). Whether that matters for forecasts is tested separately.
18.4 Frozen test (18.2 b). MAE ct night / first 14 h, Timestral loads; prior against baseline at night with block-bootstrap interval
                                          curve alone                                   with the online layer
                                          as the candidate   with the prior             as the candidate   with the prior
     summer   refitted at every origin    0.98 / 1.55                                   0.89 / 1.26
              frozen 6 Jan,  4000 steps   1.38 / 1.98        1.35 / 1.95   -2% [-4..+1]    0.98 / 1.33        0.99 / 1.34   +1% [-1..+2]
              frozen 6 Jan, 12000 steps   1.52 / 1.99        1.35 / 1.87  -11% [-21..+0]   0.98 / 1.35        0.98 / 1.35   +0% [-2..+3]
              frozen 31 Mar, 4000 steps   2.86 / 3.04        2.90 / 3.09   +1% [-1..+4]    0.95 / 1.32        0.95 / 1.32   -0% [-2..+2]
              frozen 31 Mar, 12000 steps  2.92 / 3.02        2.97 / 3.04   +2% [-1..+5]    0.96 / 1.36        0.95 / 1.36   -1% [-3..+1]
     autumn   refitted at every origin    1.69 / 1.98                                   1.59 / 1.85
              frozen 6 Jan,  4000 steps   2.26 / 2.43        2.10 / 2.29   -7% [-11..-4]   1.60 / 1.82        1.59 / 1.81   -1% [-2..+1]
              frozen 6 Jan, 12000 steps   2.10 / 2.38        2.03 / 2.21   -3% [-17..+12]  1.57 / 1.77        1.58 / 1.78   +1% [-2..+2]
              frozen 31 Mar, 4000 steps   4.06 / 4.24        3.43 / 3.66  -16% [-18..-13]  1.57 / 1.79        1.55 / 1.77   -1% [-3..-0]
              frozen 31 Mar, 12000 steps  4.17 / 4.30        3.49 / 3.67  -16% [-19..-14]  1.56 / 1.76        1.52 / 1.73   -2% [-4..-0]
  Published loads give the same picture (autumn curve alone -8% / -4% / -16% / -16%; with the layer -1% / 0% / -2% / -2%).
  Rule: better than the frozen baseline at both freeze dates, curve alone, at night, in autumn -> PASSED in sign at both step counts; the January
  effect (the headline date) is 3-7%, below the 10-25% expected, and at 12000 steps its interval includes zero.
  What the table shows beyond the rule:
  - The curve frozen on 31 March is 2.7 ct too low at night over the summer and 3.3-4.0 ct too low in autumn, with or without the prior. That is
    not the gas response (in summer the two arms differ by 0.03 ct, and gas at the origins stayed at 45-52 from April to July). Checked after
    review: the frozen curve is the curve of the freeze window's last week, and in the March fit that week's price scale is 6% below the window
    mean (10% below ten weeks earlier), which accounts for a small part only. The bulk is a rise of actual / curve through the summer: for the
    March curve 1.04 (end of March) 1.12 1.23 1.29 1.33 1.39 (August), for the January curve 0.80 0.90 0.97 1.02 1.03 1.04 over the same months.
    Both frozen curves see prices climb by a quarter to a third against a fixed curve between April and August with gas flat; the January curve
    ends near 1 only because it started 20% too high. This is the level drift of sections 9-16 again; WHY is not established here.
    (An earlier version of this line blamed the last week's scale alone. That was asserted before it was checked.)
  - With the online layer, a curve frozen in January or March is as good in autumn as one refitted at every origin (1.52-1.60 against 1.59 at
    night, 1.73-1.82 against 1.85 at 14 h) and 7-10% worse at night in summer (0.95-0.99 against 0.89). The layer does the job the prior was meant
    to do; with the layer on, the prior moves the result by -2%..+1%.
  (Housekeeping: a first launch of the refit arm used the names ga / gw / gs, which an earlier session had already used; no fits ran, and the
  layer outputs structfc_g[aws]_{known,ts}_dyn2xxh.npz were regenerated from those older runs. The refit arm of this section is ka / kw / ks.)
18.5 Under refits at every origin (18.2 c) and stability (18.2 d). Runs kw / ks / ka (GS=1), same layer, same Timestral price forecasts.
  MAE ct night / first 14 h, Timestral loads, as it is -> with the prior (change with block-bootstrap interval, night then 14 h):
                           winter                                   summer                                   autumn
     curve alone           1.41 / 1.22 -> 1.40 / 1.22  -0%, -0%     0.98 / 1.55 -> 0.99 / 1.55  +1%, +0%     1.69 / 1.98 -> 1.65 / 1.92  -2% [-6..+0], -3% [-5..-1]
     curve + its layer     0.86 / 0.93 -> 0.85 / 0.92  -1%, -1%     0.89 / 1.26 -> 0.90 / 1.26  +1%, +1%     1.59 / 1.85 -> 1.56 / 1.82  -2% [-3..-0], -2% [-3..-1]
     candidate             0.67 / 0.88 -> 0.67 / 0.87  -0% [-1..+1] 0.79 / 1.28 -> 0.79 / 1.28  +0% [-1..+1] 1.31 / 1.63 -> 1.30 / 1.62  -1% [-2..-0], -1% [-2..-0]
  Rule c (no harm, within +1% at night and at 14 h in each period): MET. Autumn gains 1% on the candidate; winter and summer do not move.
  Stability: gas share at 30 GW across the 146 origins 0.40..0.80 -> 0.63..0.67; gas response (h / 1.85) 0.55..1.25 -> 0.73..1.10, sd of its
  change between consecutive origins 0.095 -> 0.057. The difference between forecasts of the same hour from consecutive daily fits did NOT fall
  (0.44 -> 0.45 ct; 0.43 at 12000 steps): that expectation failed, the difference does not come from the split between base and gas term.
  Why the scores hardly move under refits (out/gs_fig.png, first panel): the curve is anchored at the last week of its window through the weekly
  drift of the price scale, so the two arms agree at the gas price of the origin and differ only when gas is somewhere else. A forecast two days
  ahead is made at the origin's gas price.
